| Watchdog tick / early event | The minute job is a dispatch heartbeat, not a required restart/probe every tick. Skip expensive probes/restart before due time. A new matching post-mount/WAN-connected event or explicit manual retry may request one bounded earlier readiness check after applicable settle time; a request alone does not clear outage state. Admit/coalesce before spawning or sleeping; automatic event bursts cannot bypass launch-failure backoff or provider retry_due. Use a verified monotonic/boot-relative clock so NTP corrections cannot extend/erase waits incorrectly. |
| Confirmed recovery | Once mount, NTP, IPv4 and tunnel DNS gates pass and owned process startup/healthy state meets the P1.19 sustained-stability criterion, clear outage/launch failure count, next-due marker and increased delay; restore the initial delay in full. Two-second process survival alone cannot reset repeated early-crash history. Existing dispatchers observe stability without blocking boot or creating another daemon. If an owned process is already running while outage state remains, a due/event-triggered bounded check can confirm recovery and clear the timer without restarting it; ordinary healthy/no-outage state skips those probes. The next outage starts at initial delay. Report one recovered notice; process survival remains separate from remote connectivity. Failed launch stays bounded and does not masquerade as recovery or cause a rapid crash loop. |
| Off / quarantine / uninstall-reset guard / unmount | Due timer cannot override these gates. Off removes owned runtime/maintenance integration; external kill while On instead allows recovery. Missing storage remains waiting with no bare `/opt` writes. Reboot resets RAM timer; reviewed repair/switch clears stale state after completion. Healthy owned process retains fast path through short outages. |

Store thresholds/target policy in the main CFMgr config and show waiting reason/retry due time only for enabled Cloudflared status. Do not write JFFS on watchdog ticks or create another polling daemon. P1 must verify monotonic-clock availability and exact growth/cap/launch-failure semantics; P8 must measure bounded probes/event handling. Timing defaults are proposals; nonblocking mount gating, preserved NTP/ping tests, growing retry delay, and full recovery reset are requirements.

These tunnel readiness tests are not a prerequisite for an unconfigured maintenance-only binary transaction. That path checks its own mounted storage, native transport/time/trust, artifact and transaction prerequisites, without runtime YAML/credentials/tunnel-DNS dependency or connector launch. Both paths use the same checked downloader and ownership/rollback machinery with explicitly different completion evidence. An already On but externally stopped service remains eligible for normal watchdog recovery; a maintenance transaction cannot infer On from a binary or pending marker.

### Manager updates and reinstallation

CFMgr's own self-updates are manual only through `u`/`uf`. Manager startup/menu browsing/status/hooks never check for package updates, prompt automatically, or install a package. There is no manager update daemon/cron or startup auto-update setting. Future amtm-managed updates would be a separate explicit opt-in, described below, and are not enabled by adding a launcher. Cloudflared's automatic binary updater keeps its separate feature preference/schedule; these commands do not download or update that binary.

| Trigger / result | Required manager behavior |
| --- | --- |
| `u` / explicit CLI update check | developer=true reports suppression without lookup/offer; otherwise perform the bounded local tunnel-integrity comparison, then selected-source metadata/version check offers newer compatible package with `y/n`. Explicit yes installs; current skips package replacement after integrity comparison. |
| Decline/cancel/check failure | `n`, empty confirmation, or `e` declines/cancels. Offline/timeout/invalid metadata is a check error, not proof of latest version; preserve installation and return promptly to the calling menu. No retry loop or feature/provider API calls. |
| `uf` / force / reinstall | Intentional manual force performs the local tunnel-integrity comparison, then fetches selected manager package even current with explicit target/version confirmation. developer=true additionally confirms replacement of local developer code. Full artifact validation applies; no downgrade/reset/feature reinstall or activation. |
| Startup and noninteractive feature handler | No update lookup, prompt, or package mutation. A future amtm callback, if supported/selected, is a separate external update trigger described below; ordinary feature hooks never call it. |
| Success | Report actual committed package/version, preserve selected channel and all settings/data, and reload the manager entry point safely if needed. Do not restart a healthy tunnel or rerun a feature wizard just because manager code changed. |

Reuse one manager-only transaction without Entware. On every explicit manager reinstall/update, boundedly compare safely parsed master authority with mounted runtime JSON AccountTag/TunnelID/TunnelSecret and YAML TunnelID/owned trio before deciding runtime integrity is healthy; run the same gate before any tunnel startup/activation. If `u` finds the manager package current, it may skip package replacement only after this local comparison. Matching tunnel state stays untouched: no forced restore/overwrite, JFFS resnapshot, tunnel restart or binary download, provider call, or activation change. If the user confirms a newer manager release or `uf`, transfer only that selected manager package. Repair only validated owned runtime drift/missing derived files under locks/guards/generation; preserve all other valid YAML/comments/ingress and quarantine corrupt master or invalid user YAML instead of overwriting. One internal restart is eligible only for a real validated runtime change while configured On; Off stays Off. Local file validation/reconstruction is never remote authentication success and never clears auth_fault. Manager updates never create a tunnel or run the feature wizard. A missing JFFS cert copy may be seeded only once from a trusted account/provenance-validated runtime cert during explicit schema migration/reinstall/update. Publish a release manifest/checksums binding exact package/files to selected trusted repository release/commit and expected version. Stage bounded package privately; enforce SHA256 and read embedded main-script CFMgr version as data, comparing manifest/expected version and compatible helper inventory/schema before any execution/source/install. Missing/malformed manifest, wrong hash/version, HTML or incompatible helpers fails before replacement. Then syntax-check/package validate, retain bounded JFFS recovery and atomically replace compatible package. Native HTTPS/SHA256/version/manifest parser and staging bounds remain P1 proof; never install Python/jq or bypass integrity. Force skips only same-version check.

Existing config, tunnel/credential data, logs/history/observations, backup preferences/archives, configured/enabled/maintenance/update/logging choices and foreign integrations survive live `u`/`uf`. Apply compatible data migration with recovery; missing prior config requires repair. Ordinary manager code update stays manager-only and preserves healthy services; it does not run feature installers. First installation after completed retained-config full removal is a separate reconstruction path. Developer mode is manual-file-only and default false; it suppresses manager checks/offers and future external callback/check, never switches branch or Cloudflared updater and never weakens artifact validation.

### Possible amtm support

Official amtm documentation/source reviewed on 2026-10-08 provides a clean launcher path before catalog inclusion. The [Personal scripts module](https://github.com/decoderman/amtm/blob/master/amtm_modules/personal_script.mod) supports four paths: `p` opens settings, `p1`–`p4` launch through `sh`, and `p1e`–`p4e` exit amtm afterward. It explicitly accepts scripts amtm does not officially support. Document enabling/using that feature in amtm, then adding `/jffs/scripts/cfmgr` through its UI with a user-chosen slot; preserve occupied slots. No amtm module patch or automatic registration during base install.

This launcher does not provide amtm version checking, installation, or update-catalog membership. Keep installation from the CFMgr repo and updates through its own `u`/`uf`. The official [amtm update source](https://github.com/decoderman/amtm/blob/master/amtm_modules/amtm.mod#L1000) invokes recognized scripts as `<script> amtmupdate check` for a local capability/permission gate, then `<script> amtmupdate` for an actual no-prompt update. Its own scheduler may invoke that callback. Adding the argument alone does not register CFMgr in the supported catalog; maintainers' listing/source metadata are still needed.

| Possible integration | CFMgr plan |
| --- | --- |
| Personal launcher now | Clean optional documentation route; native POSIX `sh` entry point fits. No edits to amtm-owned registry/modules, no slot takeover, and no update-policy change. Verify installed amtm/module behavior during eventual router validation. |
| Future supported update callback | Separate dispatch reuses verified manager-only transaction. Local `check` has no lookup/mutation/prompt and succeeds only for compatible external opt-in AND developer=false; actual callback is suppressed/reported when developer=true. Default opt-out, no TTY dependency, truthful exit status. |
| Catalog listing or registration hacks | Official installer/version support is a future maintainer-controlled integration. Do not force it through private catalog/update-list edits, aliases pretending to be another add-on, or a maintained amtm fork. If no clean supported route exists, keep the personal launcher and stop there. |

The callback design follows the [uiScribe example](https://github.com/AMTM-OSR/uiScribe/blob/v1.4.14/uiScribe.sh#L330); it is not implemented/advertised. Future external opt-in preserves data/locks/validation/rollback/On-Off/maintenance state, respects developer suppression and leaves Cloudflared updater independent. Source evidence is not catalog/live proof.

### Address families and provisional IPv6 support

**The current router state plus committed CFMgr configuration is authoritative for every configured DDNS/IP-Sync target.** D53 supersedes earlier stale-family retention, manual-cleanup, sole-publisher/conflict-prompt and local-cache-only no-op policies. A publishing run reconciles the complete current desired address set: update changed active addresses, remove confirmed inactive families, and correct Cloudflare-side edits automatically. Fresh setup defaults to Auto. IPv4-only and dual-stack operation, especially IPv6 enable/disable transitions, are the primary scenarios; retain symmetric IPv6-only handling as provisional coverage without requiring a force-IPv6 mode or claiming the router exposes an IPv4-disable switch.

Each feature may select allowed families independently. Import/restore retains explicit preferences. Disabling a family while its feature remains On removes that family's managed remote data; feature Off still means no operational API work and is not an implicit remote-resource deletion command. Use one firmware-native POSIX-shell resolver/cache/dispatcher with per-family results, no Python/jq/Entware runtime dependency or extra family daemon/job. Cloudflared readiness remains separate.

IPv6 stays Planned until implemented, then explicitly untested on router/provider or partially tested with actual evidence. Full live IPv6-only testing is optional; automated source-state/removal/queue/serialization safety remains required. IPv4 proof never implies IPv6 proof, and unrun live scenarios never become Pass. Keep IPv6 parser/source/provider limits visible without blocking otherwise supported independent IPv4 work.

| Current proven source state | DDNS behavior | IP-Sync behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| IPv4 and IPv6 active | Reconcile current A and AAAA values for configured names. | Exact desired set contains current IPv4 /32 and IPv6 /128 in shared or mapped lists; remove superseded or extra values. |
| IPv4 active, IPv6 disabled or confirmed absent | Maintain current A and delete managed AAAA records. | Shared list contains IPv4 only; clear any configured IPv6-only secondary list without deleting the list itself. |
| IPv6 active, IPv4 disabled or confirmed absent | Maintain current AAAA and delete managed A records. | Shared list contains IPv6 only; clear any configured IPv4-only target. This symmetric path remains provisionally supported. |
| Both confirmed inactive | Desired managed A/AAAA sets are empty; remove them if a provider path exists. If offline, leave cleanup pending and resolve current state again when transport returns. | Desired sets are empty; clear entries while retaining list IDs if reachable. Otherwise report pending cleanup, never remote-cleared success. |
| A family is unknown because inspection/discovery failed | Update/reconcile independently known family scopes, but do not infer absence or delete the unknown family. Report partial/deferred, never fully reconciled. | A shared target with unknown family state has no complete desired set: defer its replacement. Separate known-family targets can proceed. Never turn timeout/malformed/incomplete evidence into a removal command. |

Classify each family as active with a validated current public host, confirmed inactive (explicit family disable or successful complete selected-source inspection proving no eligible address), or unknown/unsupported. A missing hook argument, external lookup timeout, stale cache, malformed interface output or unavailable tool is not proof of absence. IPv4 behind NAT still needs validated current public-egress evidence; a private WAN address alone does not mean IPv4 is inactive. Freeze firmware-specific absence evidence in P1.17. Inactivity emits an explicit removal state in the queue rather than silently omitting a field. Once absence is confirmed, removal is part of normal convergence with no manual-cleanup policy or indefinite stale-address grace period.

Resolve a coherent current family/config/WAN snapshot for each publishing dispatch. Before a write, recheck current local source/config identity and relevant generation/content fingerprint; invalidate older active and removal requests when state changes. A retry cannot restore IPv6 after it was disabled, nor apply an old removal after it returned. Expired/changed/unknown evidence returns to the same shared resolver, never a duplicate helper. Reuse only still-current evidence within the coalesced run. Router state and the remote API cannot be made one atomic transaction; a change during an already-sent call queues the newest reconciliation and prevents an old result clearing newer work.

For DDNS, success requires all known desired updates AND removals for configured targets to be verified and no unresolved family/source/target work. A confirmed-inactive family with verified absence is satisfied, not a failure. Successful A update plus failed AAAA deletion remains partial/failure. No usable publication because both families are inactive is reported as cleanup complete or pending/no active address, not a successful DNS address publication; freeze the corresponding single firmware completion result in P1. Preserve callback ownership, cooldown and DNS/IP-Sync independence.

IPv6 source policy must select a usable global unicast **host address** associated with the intended WAN/router egress, not infer one from a delegated prefix or choose an arbitrary LAN/VPN interface. Validate Merlin's optional `$2`/native interface candidates against current WAN/source evidence; prefer a stable non-temporary address, reject tentative/deprecated/expired candidates, and report temporary-address churn if the supported source policy uses one. Reject unspecified, loopback, link-local, unique-local, multicast, documentation/nonpublic, IPv4-mapped and scoped `%interface` input as published IPv6 host values; validate transition/special-use cases explicitly. Compare parsed addresses canonically so compressed/expanded/uppercase spellings do not create duplicate entries or redundant writes. [IANA's IPv6 registry](https://www.iana.org/assignments/iana-ipv6-special-registry/) and [RFC 5952](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5952) guide the classifier/normalizer; source/firmware proof remains required. No automatic `/64` or delegated-prefix authorization: a router `/128` does not include LAN devices using different IPv6 addresses, including privacy addresses. Document that initial IP-Sync limitation rather than broadening Access scope.

IPv4 discovery uses bounded selected-WAN IPv4 transport; IPv6 discovery uses bounded selected-WAN IPv6 transport. Provider API transport is independent of the address family being published and must work over an available IPv4 or IPv6 path, without unconditional `curl -4` or IPv4 readiness gates. Verify native curl/DNS/CA/time/egress behavior and endpoint reachability; an IPv6-capable router alone does not prove a usable provider path. An unsupported IPv6 parser/source/provider operation reports scoped unavailability, never bypasses validation, invents IPv4, turns a working IPv4 feature Off, or masquerades as an auth failure. Use authoritative credential errors only for existing shared auth containment.

### Router-authoritative remote reconciliation

Every actual scheduled/event/manual publishing run performs a bounded current read of its configured remote targets before declaring unchanged. Compare canonical current router addresses plus configured target/TTL/proxy/other managed attributes with the complete provider state; the local last-success cache alone cannot establish equality. If they match, no provider write is needed and the verified observation can establish/refresh the local cache, including after reboot. If they differ, automatically reconcile from CFMgr's desired state even when the local IP did not change. Use the existing shared discovery/retry/daily dispatchers, with coalescing and cooldowns; no separate auth-poll daemon or API activity on ordinary menu/status/Off. The proposed shared interval remains five minutes, so external edits are corrected at the next eligible run rather than instantly.

Scope authority to configured list account/ID and managed DNS zone/name/type definitions. Changed addresses, extra entries in those owned lists, managed TTL/proxy changes and a renamed selected list are correctable drift; overwrite them from committed settings without an adoption/conflict prompt. Do not contact another account, adopt an arbitrary new list ID, delete/recreate an entire list or alter Access policies. Wrong resource type, malformed/incomplete response, invalid local configuration, auth failure and unrelated DNS records remain distinct errors/boundaries. Preserve provider attributes outside the explicitly managed field set.

A local lock only serializes CFMgr's workers. Dashboard/other-controller edits do not revoke CFMgr authority or require pausing it. If supported conditional updates are used, a conflict triggers bounded re-read/reload/reconcile against current local authority. Otherwise read/write/readback still cannot guarantee no external edit occurs afterward. Ongoing external churn remains unsatisfied/retried within fixed attempt/time/rate limits, not a manual-conflict lockout or endless tight loop. Readback confirms an observed result, not permanent remote immutability.

For DDNS, retain configured logical zone/name/type definitions and creation attributes separately from provider record IDs. Reconcile all matching managed A/AAAA records with the current host value; confirmed inactive families authorize deletion only within that managed scope. When a family returns, or its configured records were manually removed, recreate the missing configured A/AAAA definitions with current address and saved attributes, storing new IDs. This deliberately extends the reference's update-only behavior. A failed/incomplete lookup never authorizes creation/deletion. CNAME/NS conflicts and unconfigured names/types are not silently removed. After a lost create/delete response, re-enumerate the exact managed scope before retrying; never duplicate POST or treat a stale-ID 404 alone as proof the whole family is absent. Confirm both active values and removed-family absence before full success.

The documented [DNS delete](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/dns/subresources/records/methods/delete/) and [DNS create](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/dns/subresources/records/methods/create/) operations support removal and later recreation; exact permissions, native request behavior and metadata/multiple-record matching remain P1 evidence work. No new SDK or runtime package is introduced.

### IP-Sync list layout and second-list fallback

Prefer one dedicated list with the exact current host set: zero/one IPv4 /32 and zero/one IPv6 /128. The reference's maximum-one-IPv4 guard is deliberately replaced by authoritative set reconciliation; remote extra/wrong-family entries inside the configured list are removed rather than treated as foreign-data lockout. Multi-entry lists are documented, but exact mixed-family capability remains implementation evidence. If proved constraints require separate storage, use the same guided picker for a second list and map each target to a family. Ordinary IPv4-only operation needs one list.

| Boundary | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Shared versus split targets | Store list_name/list_id and optional list_name2/list_id2 with explicit family mapping. A shared list equals the current complete active-family host set. A split list equals its assigned current host or an empty set if that family is confirmed inactive. Validate configured account/ID/type and bounded complete response; current remote content is data to reconcile, not authority over router state. |
| Second-list prompt | Interactive setup/repair guides creation or selection only when verified constraints require another target. Auto-fetch returned name/ID through the same token/account picker; no manual ID requirement or automatic provider list creation. Never infer capacity from auth/network/429 errors, and a second list does not cure unsupported IPv6 itself. |
| Creation and Save | Verify distinct target IDs/account/type and available permission evidence; record selected name and mapping. Initial IPv4 normally uses primary; an existing IPv6 primary keeps its explicit mapping. e/Ctrl+C retain draft fields and leave the existing committed primary intact. Final accepted wizard Save owns its selected fields under D53. Scoped target errors do not stop an unrelated working target unless shared credential evidence requires it. |
| Background detection | Workers never prompt. Record bounded needs-second-list setup state for a genuine capacity constraint; keep the already mapped family eligible and show guidance at explicit Setup. No new recurring JFFS notices or guessed target adoption. |
| Nonempty desired set | Read current configured target, build the exact current router-authoritative set and replace if different; no retained stale sibling or extra-entry exception. Preserve unmanaged metadata, enforce saved managed name/attributes, verify complete canonical contents afterward. Publishing IPv4-only removes prior IPv6 in the same operation, and conversely for IPv6-only. |
| Empty desired set | Keep the list resource/name/ID/policy references. Do not assume PUT items:[] clears it: documentation guarantees replacement for nonempty items only. Read complete current items, PATCH-remove their provider-returned value spellings in bounded batches, then verify empty contents/count. Already-empty contents need no item-removal call, but changed managed name/attributes still need metadata reconciliation (for example PUT with items omitted). Full no-op requires both verified empty contents and matching managed metadata. Canonical comparison never substitutes a different removal spelling without proof. Partial removal remains pending; unsupported clear capability is an explicit limitation, never stale retention reported as success. |
| Queue and partial outcomes | Queue active-address and confirmed-inactive removal states per family/target with source/config/credential generations and current-state checks. Merge newer events without losing removal intent; serialize same-target writes and keep sibling independent outcomes. Old late replies cannot satisfy current work. Retry reloads latest desired state and reads current remote state before further writes, never blindly replays a stale IP or clear operation. |
| Shared-to-split migration | Before final Save validate second target and mapping; cancellation preserves the old committed setup. Quiesce owned old-target requests, atomically commit new mapping/generation, then automatically converge primary to only its assigned current family and secondary to its own current family. Remove misplaced/stale entries from primary rather than retaining them for manual cleanup. Two remote writes are not atomic: record independent pending outcomes and resume from current state. The earlier split-cleanup-pending exception is superseded, not a runtime mode to implement. |
| Policy wiring and scope | Explain that the user must reference the second list in the intended Access policy; no automatic Include/Require/Exclude edits. Clearing items preserves list identity. Readback does not prove end-user authorization. Backup/restore retains both target definitions/family mapping and user preferences, while live queues/caches rebuild from current router state. |

Cloudflare documents nonempty replacement through [list update](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/zero_trust/subresources/gateway/subresources/lists/methods/update/) and item removal through [list PATCH](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/zero_trust/subresources/gateway/subresources/lists/methods/edit/). Prove complete pagination, native bounded removal and readback; never delete/recreate a list merely to empty it.

### Guided IP-Sync list discovery and name input

Primary and conditional second-list setup must guide the user from an API token and account ID to a validated list without requiring them to locate/type its ID. Hold credentials privately in the existing reviewed setup transaction, perform explicit bounded read-only discovery, and show list names with stable numbering. Choose a display cap of **10**. If the account has at most 10 eligible IP lists, accept a displayed number or exact case-sensitive list name. If more exist, show at most the first 10 as a clearly labelled preview and switch to exact-name entry; search beyond the displayed subset. Tell the user that case and spaces matter, quotes are optional, and an empty/absent list may be created in the dashboard followed by Refresh. Do not create provider lists automatically or contact the API from ordinary status/launch.

Use [Zero Trust Gateway list enumeration](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/zero_trust/subresources/gateway/subresources/lists/methods/list/), distinct from account Rules Lists. Its current docs describe fetch-all, with optional type and case-insensitive substring name filtering; such a filter is only a candidate search, never exact identity proof. Match the returned decoded name locally with exact case-sensitive equality. Prefer type-filtered enumeration plus local comparison, or a separately URL-encoded search value; never interpolate a name into filter-expression syntax without proven escaping. If such escaping is unsupported, use complete enumeration/local matching. Ten is only a display limit, never a lookup limit. Validate response success/type/completeness and all candidates; if actual supported API pagination exists, traverse it within frozen bounds. Do not assume list-items pagination applies to list enumeration. Incomplete/truncated/oversized results, unexplained total-count mismatch, repeated pages or exhausted budgets mean lookup incomplete/deferred, never not found or a guessed first match. Version-specific enumeration/completeness behavior is P1.17 evidence work. [List details](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/zero_trust/subresources/gateway/subresources/lists/methods/get/) and [paginated list items](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/zero_trust/subresources/gateway/subresources/lists/subresources/items/methods/list/) provide separate target/content checks.

| Input / setup edge | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Number or exact name | Only at the initial numbered-picker prompt with at most 10 eligible lists does a number select the stable displayed snapshot; re-fetch/revalidate the selected target before Save. Out-of-range numbers return a helpful retry. With more than 10 lists OR after entering the exact-name retry loop, numeric input is always a literal-name candidate, never a preview index regardless of list count. In the numbered picker, if numeric input also identifies a literal list name, clarify rather than guess; an explicitly quoted numeric name uses name lookup. Exact-name search includes eligible undisplayed lists. Zero exact matches after complete lookup prints List not found and immediately repeats Enter the exact list name (e to exit):, retaining token/account until valid selection or exit; multiple exact matches require explicit disambiguation or stop, never first-match adoption. |
| Spaces and optional quotes | Read literal input with POSIX-safe input handling (for example IFS= read -r), preserving internal spaces/backslashes/case; never eval/source or interpolate it into shell code. One balanced surrounding single- or double-quote pair may be input syntax. First retain the raw name candidate and also consider one unwrapped candidate; if both refer to different real lists, ask which is intended. Do not strip interior quotes/apostrophes or repeatedly unwrap; unmatched/literal quote characters must not become shell syntax. Reject unsupported control characters with a clear message. |
| API encoding | User wrapper quotes are not HTTP syntax. Encode the selected exact string once for its actual transport: native URL encoding for query values, validated JSON string escaping only for JSON bodies. Shell-quote arguments without adding literal quote wrappers to the name. Preserve spaces, apostrophes, ampersands, percent signs, backslashes and supported Unicode correctly; no manual double wrapping, double escaping or shell expansion. API operations target returned ID, not a guessed name-derived URL. |
| Confirm and Save | Show selected provider-returned name/ID and intended family mapping in the normal final review, without token output. Validate correct account/ID/IP type and complete bounded data; selected target contents/renames are correctable drift, not grounds for conflict prompts. Atomically save accepted wizard name/ID/mapping and reconcile desired state after Save. Reject same-ID secondary, missing whole target, wrong account/type or malformed/incomplete response; never guess identity. Read success alone is not write-permission or Access-policy proof. |
| Empty, limited or cancelled discovery | Explain required account/token list-read permissions with dashboard guidance; distinguish invalid credentials, denied enumeration, throttle and transport failure. Reuse existing cooldown/auth containment, no guessed capacity fallback. Cancellation/failed discovery keeps previously working config and completed native setup draft fields; e exits Setup, Ctrl+C exits safely, next Setup resumes. A clearly advanced manual-ID validation path may remain for existing known targets where listing is unavailable; no requirement to use it in normal setup. Both lists share the same picker/validation logic. |

### DDNS and Access parity

| Behavior | Required implementation rule |
| --- | --- |
| Address discovery | One shared native resolver returns current active/confirmed-inactive/unknown family states. Validate hook candidates/WAN/source; NAT-aware IPv4 and selected global-host IPv6. Emit removal intent for proven disable/absence, never infer absence from failed lookup or duplicate discovery code. |
| IPv6 | Provisional support focuses on dual-stack and IPv6 disable/removal/return beside IPv4. Retain symmetric IPv6-only behavior without a forced-mode requirement. Current source/config is authoritative; unknown capability is deferred, not proven absence. Label exact testing coverage. |
| DNS records | Update every matching managed A/AAAA to current family value and desired TTL/proxy; delete managed records for confirmed inactive families. Preserve configured logical definitions/attributes separately from transient IDs so active missing definitions can be recreated. No guessed creation from lookup failure or deletion of unrelated name/type conflicts. D53 deliberately extends reference update-only behavior. |
| Transport | Preserve bounded DNS/IP HTTP calls (current connect timeout 5 seconds, total 20 seconds). Validate HTTP/API results and handle record pagination explicitly. |
| Completion | One owned firmware result: all desired managed updates/removals verified, no unknown/failed target work before full success. Confirmed absence already reflected remotely is satisfied. Active-family update with failed inactive-family deletion is partial/failure; no-address cleanup is reported separately from usable DDNS publication. |
| Sync status/history | Record confirmed address publication, removal/empty cleanup and unchanged verification separately per family/target. Cache cannot mask remote drift or unknown source state. Partial update/delete/readback remains pending; no success for merely attempted or queued work. Native/logging failure does not change provider outcome. |
| Access independence | Forward validated per-family observations asynchronously, including an IPv6-only result. List failure never delays/fails DDNS completion; DNS failure never suppresses valid source observations. |
| IP-Sync setup isolation | Configure account/list/token independently while referencing the shared IPv4/IPv6 policy. Prefer an enabled DDNS producer's valid observation, including when a subsequent DNS write fails. Without enabled DDNS, execute only the shared resolver and IP-Sync list work; no DNS credentials, DDNS completion call or implicit DDNS enablement. |
| List safety | Validate configured account/ID/IP type and bounded complete response. Exact current desired active-family set replaces differing addresses/extra entries inside that list; remote edits do not cause a content-conflict prompt. Confirmed inactive family removed, empty target cleared through supported same-ID item removal. Unknown source cannot authorize removal or a complete shared-list replacement. Never adopt another list or change policy. |
| API work | Bounded current reads, no-op only on complete canonical desired/remote match; otherwise update/remove/clear then verify. Saved managed attributes win over remote edits, unmanaged metadata preserved. Explicit read-only target check remains GET-only even configured Off: no publication/removal/queue/cache/history/enable/discovery. Explicit candidates report only tested scope; omitted family is not absence evidence. Shared cooldown/saved-credential auth containment still applies. |
| Queue | Merge newest per-family/target address OR confirmed-inactive removal state. Coherent source/config generations prevent earlier addresses or clears overwriting current intent. Serialize target writers, preserve child lock lifetime, four-attempt and aggregate bounds; late replies never clear newer work. |
| Retry | Reload/revalidate latest source/config snapshot and read current remote state before retrying. Shared resolver refreshes changed/expired/unknown evidence; IP-Sync never calls DNS or copies discovery logic. Disable invalidates old-address requests; re-enable invalidates old clear requests. Reboot/restore starts fresh, with cooldown respected and no historical replay. |
| Disabled module | Routine dispatch with missing/disabled Access configuration is a no-op. Explicit configured-Off target check is the narrow provider-read-only exception above; it cannot enable/publish. No dependency installation for unselected features. |
| Policy meaning | Publishing the list does not itself create a bypass policy. Preserve existing Access policy behavior; the wizard must not silently change authorization policies. |

The list update uses Cloudflare's [Zero Trust list API](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/zero_trust/subresources/gateway/subresources/lists/methods/update/). DNS edits follow the [DNS record API](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/dns/subresources/records/methods/edit/). Validate required permissions against the selected operations during implementation.

### Confirmed native desired state and shared provider outcomes

| Observation / outcome | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Routine unchanged native observation | Every eligible publish run reads the complete configured remote state, even unchanged local IP. Compare to current source/config desired active sets and removals. Equality skips writes and refreshes boot-local evidence; cache equality alone never skips remote verification. No extra auth polling or status/menu calls. |
| Changed settings / unsatisfied work | Current settings/family/target/credential content change invalidates affected keys and queues even without a manual revision bump. Remote differences auto-reconcile from current authority. Partial add/delete/clear or ambiguous reply never advances full success; retain only latest still-applicable desired work. |
| Reboot / restore / first target/family use | Bootstrap current remote state through bounded target/readback checks before relying on no-op cache. A complete matching current readback may establish satisfied state, distinguished from a new successful publication; only successful full write/required readback advances publication history. Restored historical snapshots are advisory history, never live success/auth/health. Explicit setup/On/credential repair may verify auth/target read-only even same IP; no needless write when desired state already matches. |
| Explicit target refresh / external changes | Scheduled/coalesced event/manual publishing automatically reads and corrects managed remote drift; explicit update/publish uses the same contract. Same IP never hides changed Cloudflare contents. Recreate only configured missing DNS definitions; a deleted whole list still needs configured target repair, not guessed new identity. Detection bound follows existing dispatcher/cooldown, not instant monitoring. |
| Verified provider throttling | Retain configured/enabled On, Cloudflared maintenance activation and operational hooks/jobs. Record scoped retry_due and latest eligible pending work; native warning plus own installed/ready file, redacted. Honor valid Cloudflare Retry-After/reset delay; proposed fallback 300 seconds only without usable hint, never retry earlier than valid server delay. Shared account/credential/endpoint scope prevents linked token/worker bypass; scope must follow actual provider evidence, not string equality. |
| Due dispatch / coalescing | Nonblocking existing watchdog/daily DDNS/native retry/shared discovery handlers check due under locks/generation guards; merge newest compatible per-family/target desired state without dropping sibling work or replaying stale observations. If needed one exact owned DDNS retry job exists only while valid On, removed by Off/auth-Off. No minute-long manager sleep, launch/boot hang, polling daemon, manager-update or backup cron. Exact retry job/clock/delay/scope contracts Pending P1. |
| Transient transport/time/DNS/TLS/5xx | Keep On waiting/retry under existing bounded readiness/capped retry gates; distinguish from authoritative bad credentials. No token prompt/public Off from network outage, generic status or zero connections. No TLS bypass. |
| Initial reviewed setup / throttle | Complete validated runtime setup may publish reviewed On/hooks, then throttle/network leaves On waiting. Fresh B without confirmed UUID/complete credentials stays inactive/incomplete with requested intent advisory only. Failed/incomplete or unknown NEW create directs manual Zero Trust check/deletion and explicit retry after correction; no auto-create from a due job or returning mount. Runtime retry for an already known configured tunnel remains unchanged. |
| AUTHORITATIVE bad credential / revocation / operation permission | Source-tested definitive rejection writes durable non-secret auth_fault/reason/typed credential ref/prior-requested On and new affected generation BEFORE public Off/quiescence/unhooking. Native feature removes only own operational jobs/hooks/quiesces work; Cloudflared public Off also clears maintenance_enabled/removes ALL runtime/watchdog/updater integration. Preserve completed local configured state where valid, keys/identity/binary/settings/log prefs/files/rules and Scribe independent rotation. No blanket kill or remote recreation. |
| Auth-Off cleanup failure / inflight native call | Close activation/generation gate first, bounded owned TERM/wait/recheck; targeted KILL only for confirmed auth containment of still-verified own connector, same scoped exception as corruption. Unknown ownership/surviving process retains Off/guard+cleanup-required, no fully-stopped claim/replacement/restart. Native already-sent remote request cannot be undone; new generation blocks subsequent/queued/stale-response state writes, full remote proof required for any historical success, never clearing new fault/reenabling. |
| Non-authoritative / identity error | Live PID/zero connections/start failure/generic HTTP403/resource404/raw log text alone does not prove invalid credentials. Distinguish permission/auth codes+operation evidence from resource/identity, transient/throttle errors; unsupported ambiguity stays bounded waiting/repair diagnosis, not guessed Off or credential loop. Resource/identity repair uses reviewed setup/import without changing UUID during credential repair. |
| Shared credential impact | Proven global revocation may fault verified dependents referencing that shared typed credential; operation-only missing permission affects only verified failing feature/operation. Sharing token string does not authorize Off of unrelated working feature. Account/endpoint rate scope may span references only with verified evidence. |

Cloudflare's [API limits guidance](https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/api/reference/limits/) documents Retry-After and endpoint-specific limits; the five-minute fallback is a proposed local default, not a universal provider promise. Version-specific Cloudflared startup/reconnect/RPC rejection classifier is Pending P1: a PID may survive and retry despite rejection, and connection metrics alone cannot explain it. Watchdog/start needs source-tested bounded evidence/health checks independent of Scribe, using private bounded RAM failure/status signals when custom logging off (no persistent hidden history, raw debug/syslog stream or new polling daemon/tool dependency). Emit only classified redacted notices to native messages and own eligible file; never raw tokens/API bodies. API/runtime classifier, native parser/header/clock/scope, scheduler and evidence bounds require fixtures/source proof before implementation.

For split IP-Sync, apply authoritative failures at the evidenced target/credential scope. A list-specific permission or identity fault blocks only that target, invalidates its queued/success state and leaves the feature On while another configured target can operate. Global credential rejection still applies public auth-Off to every verified affected target/feature. Keep target fault/prior intent durable without per-run JFFS churn and reuse repair grouping. Freeze the target-versus-feature fault schema in P1.17; generic feature-level auth-Off rows apply only when that is the proven scope.

### Minimal credential repair and persisted auth faults

| Trigger / choice | Required flow |
| --- | --- |
| First definitive auth failure after reviewed setup/On | Complete fault/public-Off containment, then interactive flow goes directly to relevant credential entry ONLY. No repeated account/record/routing wizard or tunnel creation. Save replacement atomically with typed refs/other settings preserved; validated accepted repair rehooks/resumes only prior faulted/requested On work through existing gates. |
| Repeated failure / transient response | Another confirmed auth rejection returns to that credential prompt after safe Off/fault update. e/Ctrl+C cancels safely with affected Off and fault retained; no forced infinite UI loop. Verified throttle/network wait retains On, returns menu/deferred work and never requests new token merely for 429. |
| Next interactive launch | After local integrity/removal checks, persisted local auth faults ask exact `Fix invalid credentials now? [Y/n]`; blank=y, n returns normal menu with persistent redacted warning, invalid input reprompts. Conditional main fx opens same repair anytime. Noninteractive hook/cron never prompts. No provider check/update on ordinary healthy launch. |
| Distinct credential grouping | Prompt each distinct invalid compatible typed record once, serially; shared DDNS/IP-Sync reference repair atomically updates and rereads verified dependent groups after Save. No incompatible-type dedupe or string-equality scope inference. Repeated definitive rejection may re-enter same credential; preserve unrelated settings/features. |
| Eligible restoration of intent | Fault stores prior/requested On separately from later intentional user Off. Accepted repair/fx or explicit On authorizes validation/retry only for still-requested faulted work; intentionally user-Off features stay Off even when shared credential replaced. Cloudflared repair reinstates eligible prior maintenance/update choices only through its On contract, not a hidden startup flag. |
| Manual authoritative config edit | Safely parse/validate changed credentials through same reconciliation; already On eligible runtime change may coalesce one internal restart. Otherwise Off stays Off until accepted repair/fx/explicit On authorizes retry; edited file presence alone never auto-enables. No source/eval or secret display. |
| Runtime versus management failure | Ask only actual failed typed credential. Missing B JSON rebuilds from main authority; rejected unchanged saved secret remains faulted, do not regenerate/restart in a loop. Accepted explicit B repair may obtain management cert via browser login then fetch same-UUID JSON to private staging and validate identity. Management login alone is insufficient. Failed creation stays inactive/manual cleanup D43; unrecoverable existing runtime offers separately reviewed replacement D44, never silent new UUID. |
| A existing token recovery | Direct user to existing remotely managed tunnel's dashboard connector token (Overview/Add a replica; refresh only deliberately). Save token privately and validate expected identity/account with supported evidence before retry; another tunnel's token is reviewed setup, not credential repair. No service-install command execution, automatic token rotation or dashboard assumption for local B. |
| B existing-UUID retrieval | On accepted repair, use validated same-account cert or explicit guarded browser login, then `cloudflared tunnel token --cred-file <fresh-private-staging.json> <existing-UUID>`. No new create or token stdout logging. Validate complete returned JSON/account/UUID, atomically save current secret to master/derived JSON and retry only eligible prior faulted On work. File/fetch success is not runtime authentication: definitive rejection of the retrieved credentials still preserves auth fault/Off and may lead to the reviewed replacement offer after recoverable account/permission issues are ruled out. Unsupported old tunnel, deleted resource or provider permission rejection leaves fault/Off; wrong account/permission is corrected first. Exact installed version/local-mode/provider capability Pending P1. |
| Recovery cannot succeed / replacement offer | After supported same-UUID recovery/import/whole-backup options are exhausted or conclusively unavailable, offer `Create replacement tunnel? [y/N]`; blank/n/e leaves affected Off. Explain old ID and manual Zero Trust deletion, no remote automatic deletion. Acceptance enters reviewed setup/Save, never runs create inside background/fx token loop. Preserve FULL validated user YAML/ingress and owned private .bak before patching new owned trio; uncertain YAML remains available for manual re-patch. Network/throttle/mount loss/repairable permission is not proof recreation needed. One managed connector only, no automatic enable of intentional Off. |
| Restored fault data / signals | Archived non-secret fault/reason/intent is advisory only; actual saved Off remains Off, active auth/runtime guards/queues/cooldowns never imported live. Revalidate explicitly in current manager, no auto-On from archive repair intent. Shared Save/e/Ctrl+C/generation/owned stop/hook-preservation contracts apply to replacement/rehook, with durable fault before background work can respawn. |

The [official dashboard token guide](https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/networks/connectors/cloudflare-tunnel/configure-tunnels/remote-tunnel-permissions/) documents obtaining or refreshing the connector token for an existing remotely managed tunnel. The [CLI token command](https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflared/blob/master/cmd/cloudflared/tunnel/subcommands.go) documents --cred-file JSON output for an existing UUID and warns that tunnels must have been created since cloudflared 2022.3.0. Its [account-certificate client and retrieval implementation](https://github.com/cloudflare/cloudflared/blob/master/cmd/cloudflared/tunnel/subcommand_context.go) fetches/parses the current token; login itself only obtains the management cert. This establishes a source-supported same-UUID repair route, not universal installed/local-tunnel compatibility or remote success. P1 must prove the selected version/tunnel/provider permission path; no new API-token wizard or automatic A/B conversion.

### Shared public address resolver

Implement one bundled POSIX/firmware helper for WAN selection, IPv4/IPv6 host parsing/validation, independent family source policy and bounded discovery. Missing/failed IPv4 never discards valid IPv6, or vice versa. Both features call this same helper; DDNS adds DNS work/completion, IP-Sync adds list work only. No DDNS credentials/configuration, Entware, Python or duplicated resolver/classifier/cache. The NAT table below applies only to IPv4; IPv6 follows the selected-WAN global-host/lifetime policy above.

Use the selected WAN's verified native address, normally `wan<N>_ipaddr`, with actual firmware/interface/PPPoE mapping proved in P1. Classify standards-defined ranges: [RFC 6598](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6598) allocates `100.64.0.0/10` for shared space; [IANA's IPv4 special-purpose registry](https://www.iana.org/assignments/iana-ipv4-special-registry/) identifies private and other special-use ranges. These are universal protocol constants, never an ISP's customer/public CIDR or an account-specific address. Handle registry exceptions correctly rather than blindly treating every special-purpose prefix as non-public.

The inspected Skynet `Is_PrivateIP()` in `IPSet_ASUS/firewall.sh` classifies non-public addresses and warns about bridge mode/CG-NAT; it does not compare an external address or prove carrier ownership. Non-public WAN or an external mismatch determines that external discovery is needed. Record `nat_state` as true/false/unknown with its evidence/reason; identify shared-range indication separately from private/double-NAT or observed mismatch. Do not claim a universal carrier-NAT proof or turn a failed check into false. CGNAT/upstream NAT does not prevent publishing an externally observed public IPv4, but does not make inbound IPv4 services reachable.

| WAN / evidence | Shared resolution behavior |
| --- | --- |
| RFC 6598 shared-space WAN | Indicate likely CGNAT/shared addressing and use bounded external IPv4 discovery. Never publish the router's shared address. |
| RFC 1918 private WAN | Indicate upstream NAT/private WAN and use the same external discovery path. Do not claim the carrier rather than a local modem owns it. |
| Valid public WAN with matching fresh external evidence | Infer no address translation observed; return the native WAN address. Reuse a still-valid direct-WAN observation without another HTTP request. |
| Valid public WAN differs from correctly routed external IPv4 | Indicate observed upstream translation/mismatch and use the validated external result. This covers NAT arrangements a private/shared-range-only test would miss. |
| New/changed WAN, boot, expired cache or source-policy change | Revalidate through bounded selected-WAN external observation before assigning direct/NAT state. A public-looking address alone is insufficient proof of no upstream NAT. |
| Disconnected/invalid WAN, failed external check, invalid/non-public/oversized discovery reply or unverifiable egress | Successful complete inspection proving IPv4 disabled/absent emits inactive/removal. Failed/incomplete inspection or unverified public egress emits unknown, never a guessed removal or private/shared fallback. Known independent IPv6 scopes may proceed; a shared list with unknown family state defers complete replacement. |

IPv4 discovery requests use IPv4 and verified selected-WAN egress; IPv6 discovery uses IPv6 and its verified egress. VPN/proxy/another WAN cannot silently supply either published address. Shared config stores bounded ordered per-family endpoints and time/size limits. Provider API transport is independent and may use either reachable family; never force all API calls to IPv4. Prove native source/egress/tool support without dependencies or TLS bypass.

| Producer / trigger | Dispatch and optimization contract |
| --- | --- |
| Enabled DDNS invocation | Resolve current family states once, forward immutable current observations/removal intent to IP-Sync, then independently reconcile DNS updates/deletions and completion. List failure never changes DNS outcome, DNS failure never discards valid router source evidence. |
| IP-Sync without enabled DDNS | Use only the shared resolver/current family snapshot then list reconciliation, including removal/clear. No DDNS setup, DNS record lookup/write or firmware completion callback. |
| Reviewed first enable, boot/recovery/restore, matching WAN-connected/reconnected, IPv6 address/prefix renewal or explicit update/publish | Request a coalesced detached discovery under native feature/source/transport gates, independent of Cloudflared readiness. Manager launch, status and local hook self-heal remain read-only/no-network; a completed enable transaction schedules work rather than blocking the menu on HTTP. |
| Shared discovery cron | One job while either native feature is On, proposed every five minutes. Detect IPv4 egress and IPv6 host/prefix/lifetime/disable changes even with unchanged IPv4 NVRAM, then reconcile configured remote targets even if local addresses are unchanged. Coalesce overlapping requests; preserve daily DDNS and bounded retry scheduling, no duplicate per-family jobs. |
| Saved-state retry / both native features Off | Before retry, refresh current source/config through shared helper if needed, then reconcile current remote state. Saved addresses or removals are never blindly replayed. Both Off removes shared operational integration; disabling one feature leaves the other independent. |

Use one bounded private RAM cache and coalesced resolver lock with separate family records. Key each by boot, selected WAN, native address/interface/prefix/source identity, policy/config generation and monotonic freshness; IPv6 freshness never exceeds source lifetime. Invalidate affected family records on WAN/source/address/prefix/lifetime changes, preserving still-compatible sibling observations. Recheck generation/egress before dispatch; discard late replies and preserve newer requests. Expire/recheck IPv4 NAT and IPv6 source evidence even with unchanged IPv4 NVRAM. Share snapshots across features/cron/manual overlap; no JFFS tick/cache writes. Queue merge/readback/success stays per family/target. Prove one source-discovery sequence per shared current family observation, no duplicate source HTTP calls within a coalesced run (required provider reads remain), and provisional IPv6-only standalone recovery and aggregate limits across both families/fallbacks. Bounds and native routing proof remain P1 work.

## Scribe logging and rotation

### What is established

The primary reference writes tunnel output to `/opt/var/log/cloudflared.log` and emits lifecycle/DDNS/Access messages to syslog. It does not include its active Scribe rotation policy.

| Current producer | Current logging path | CFMgr treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cloudflared` connector | Direct application logfile `/opt/var/log/cloudflared.log`; background console output is suppressed. | Import existing log/history when eligible, then use `cloudflared.log` only with usable Scribe. Detailed daemon output stays in this file and is never mirrored to system messages. |
| Watchdog/shared lifecycle | `logger` with `cloudflared-watchdog` or shared `cloudflared-control` tag. | Preserve relevant watchdog, tunnel start/stop, NTP/ntpd, ping/readiness, and lifecycle result notices in native system messages independently of custom file logging. No separate manager logfile. |
| Binary updater | `logger` with `cloudflared-update` tag. | Preserve redacted updater result/activity notices in native system messages; distinguish these brief manager events from the daemon's detailed application stream. |
| DDNS | `logger` with `DDNS-CF`; also prints when invoked in a terminal. | Preserve native messages, interactive results, and firmware completion. Add separate Scribe-gated `ddns.log` for redacted sync/result events with a fixed size policy; no rotation tuning. |
| Access helper / IP-Sync | Syslog with `CF-Access-IP`, plus console messages; hooks redirect its output. | Preserve native queue/retry/API notices and add separate Scribe-gated `ip-sync.log` for redacted sync/result events with a fixed size policy; no rotation tuning. |

The captured Scribe setting names `/tmp/syslog.log` as the system-log path. Actual destinations/retention still depend on its active syslog-ng and logrotate rules; do not infer those rules from the tags or filename alone.

The separate captured `jffs/scripts/scribe` identifies AMTM-OSR Scribe and defines `/opt/etc/syslog-ng.conf`, `/opt/etc/syslog-ng.d`, `/opt/etc/logrotate.conf`, and `/opt/etc/logrotate.d` (lines 149–211). It checks `syslog-ng` process/config state; its `LogRotate` path locks `/tmp/scribeLogRotate.flock` and runs `/opt/sbin/logrotate /opt/etc/logrotate.conf` (lines 1539–1551 and 1712–1746). Captured `jffs/addons/scribe.d/config` selects `/tmp/syslog.log`; captured `post-mount` registers Scribe rotation at 00:05 when Entware is available.

Treat Scribe script/package presence as a detection signal, not proof of a configured logging system. Its fallback discovery concerns syslog-ng destinations; the three direct CFMgr-owned logfiles each need an explicit owned policy. Native messages remain firmware-managed, with no CFMgr syslog-ng routing changes. The maintained [Scribe project](https://github.com/AMTM-OSR/scribe) provides the upstream integration reference.

### Logging state model

CFMgr manages three separate custom files: cloudflared's application logfile, DDNS sync/result events, and IP-Sync sync/result events. Each integration requires that feature to be configured, custom logging requested, verified Entware storage, and usable Scribe/logrotate. Files share the approved log directory but have separate ownership, preferences, and rotation rules. Cloudflared alone has user-tunable rotation; DDNS/IP-Sync use fixed defaults.

Native system messages are a separate path and do not require Scribe. DDNS/IP-Sync send redacted events to system messages and, when available, their own file. Cloudflared's detailed daemon output goes only to its own file; never pipe it into syslog. Brief manager updater/watchdog, tunnel start/stop, NTP/ntpd, ping/readiness, and lifecycle notices still use system messages when relevant. Custom logging off/unavailable does not mute those notices.

| Detected state | CFMgr behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Feature never configured | Skip its automatic Scribe detection/repair; create no logfile/rule or logging controls. Its own Scribe re-detect becomes available only after dedicated setup. Base install remains passive. |
| Scribe absent or incomplete/unusable | Custom file logging unavailable: no owned file writes/fragments, omit daemon logfile flags, and suppress background daemon stdout/stderr. Explain failed checks in interactive status. Preserve native DDNS/IP-Sync and relevant manager notices; feature setup/operation can proceed without Scribe. |
| First fresh feature setup completes; Scribe/logrotate ready | Automatically stage/save and validate only that feature's default file policy, then enable it. Incomplete advanced Cloudflared preparation does not activate logging/rules. Completed Cloudflared starts with size-based defaults and offers later advanced tuning; DDNS/IP-Sync have no size/count/compression questions or tuning menu. |
| Existing/imported/restored policy | Preserve preferences, Cloudflared custom values and saved On/Off/maintenance state. Repeat setup/launch/repair/restore never resets defaults or turns Off back On. |
| Scribe ready and that feature's custom logging enabled | Enable only its private file and validated owned rotation fragment. Preserve other features and unrelated logging configuration. |
| Scribe base ready; owned fragment/include missing or stale | When that configured feature's custom logging preference is on, regenerate only its owned integration from the saved cloudflared policy or versioned fixed DDNS/IP-Sync policy, validate, and report success. Missing owned rules must not be confused with an unusable Scribe base. |
| Custom logging preference inactive / explicitly uninstalled | No owned file output or automatic check/self-heal. Uninstall logging removes found owned logs/rotations and eligible exact owned rule/include, rather than offering a separate temporary stop-logging toggle. Preserve policy values/opt-out and native messages; re-detect alone cannot reinstall/enable. |
| Service Off / Stop | No new owned service/API events; preserve logging preference, files/rotations/rules. Scribe/logrotate continues independent rotation, with no CFMgr stop/start/restart of its daemon/job/global policies. Logging checks cannot enable the service. |
| Scribe becomes unavailable | Effective file output unavailable; preserve requested preference/history and native messages. Coordinate an eligible owned Cloudflared controller restart without logfile or quiesce native job writer as needed; no invented native daemon/forced sync. A still-running writer is not proof of ready Scribe. Explicit Uninstall logging works immediately without re-detect/heal, follows cleanup contract below and saves opt-out. |
| Classified provider outcome / auth-Off notice | Redacted throttle/auth notices go native and to that feature's installed/requested/ready file, including final classified auth-Off diagnostic before or as quiescence commits; never start service or enable logging to write notice. Logging uninstall guard/opt-out suppresses file writes. Scribe rotation remains independent of service enable. Bounded RAM runtime evidence/classifier works without Scribe, not raw log/history capture. |

Custom-log readiness requires verified Entware, installed/configured Scribe, usable syslog-ng/logrotate executables and base configurations, active syslog-ng as required by Scribe, a functioning Scribe rotation schedule, and validated inclusion of owned fragments. Probe the base prerequisites separately from generated files so missing owned rules are repairable. Native messages need no Scribe-readiness check. Detection is read-only; repair does not reinstall Scribe or replace global configuration.

The captured Scribe reinstall calls uninstall before install (lines 3106–3119); uninstall removes `/opt/etc/logrotate.conf` and the entire fragment directory (lines 2151–2161). Scribe also regenerates `A01global` (lines 781–800). Keep CFMgr preferences/cloudflared policy in JFFS and versioned fixed-policy metadata with the manager, then generate separate owned fragments after completed reinstall instead of relying on surviving files or editing `A01global`.

### Proposed owned logging integration

| Item | Planned behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Files and routing | `/opt/var/log/cfmgr/cloudflared.log`, `ddns.log`, and `ip-sync.log` are separate owned files. Detailed cloudflared output stays file-only. DDNS/IP-Sync redacted events also go to native messages; brief manager maintenance/readiness notices stay native. No separate manager file or new syslog-ng routes. |
| Cloudflared file | Retain/migrate existing application log and rotations with recoverable staging. Use validated daemon logfile arguments, not Scribe auto-discovery or a pipe into syslog. |
| DDNS/IP-Sync event files | Append bounded structured lines containing time, feature/target identity, outcome, and verified synced IP where relevant. Never log tokens, certificates, credential bodies, or full API responses. Serialize owned writes; short-lived append/open/close avoids assuming daemon-style reopen semantics. Logging errors do not fail API work or Merlin completion. |
| Rotation ownership | One non-A01global fragment per feature under included logrotate.d; Scribe alone manages scheduler/rotation. Service Stop/Off retains its rule/history and independent rotation. Explicit logging uninstall removes only selected owned setup/data; no Scribe daemon/job/global-policy control or deletion of another feature's rule/shared history. |
| Cloudflared policy | Generate from validated main-config settings below and explicitly override inherited policy where needed. |
| DDNS/IP-Sync policy | Generate each fixed size-based policy from the manager's versioned defaults below. No custom size/count/time/compression options in their menus or editable schema; save preference/policy version, not user overrides. Status can show the actual rule. |
| Open-file handling | Verify cloudflared log-reopen behavior before selecting rename/reopen or `copytruncate`; document the small loss window if copytruncate is required. A syslog-ng HUP does not prove the daemon reopened its file. Validate concurrent short-lived DDNS/IP-Sync append/rotation behavior separately. |
| Validation and backup | Validate the active include chain in logrotate debug mode during setup/repair, including duplicate/nested matches. Agree a non-deadlocking writer/rotation/snapshot strategy for eligible owned log backups; never include the whole router syslog. |

### Rotation settings in the main configuration

These are proposed cloudflared setting names/defaults for `/jffs/addons/CFMgr.d/config`; serialization and installed-tool support remain P1/P6 work. Apply basic size-based rotation only when first fresh cloudflared setup completes with ready Scribe, whether token or advanced mode. Keep size/count/time/age/compression tuning in the Cloudflared logging submenu, available with configured tunnel and ready Scribe; do not ask those advanced questions in initial tunnel setup. Preserve imported/saved policy and later user changes. These tuning fields never control DDNS/IP-Sync or native system messages. The supplied launch helper sets a logfile but contains no size threshold, and active rotation rules are absent from that snapshot; `10M` below remains proposed until current-policy evidence confirms the desired size.

| Setting | Proposed fresh-setup default | User option / behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `logging_enabled` | On for first cloudflared setup with ready Scribe; otherwise off | Saved custom-file installation/output preference, independent of service On/Off and Scribe's rotation schedule. Uninstall logging saves false; explicit reviewed Install/enable needed after opt-out. Keep readiness separate. |
| `logrotate_mode` | Size | Size-based rotation, or optional time-based rotation with a size threshold. |
| `logrotate_size` | `10M` | Validated positive threshold with units; required for size mode. |
| `logrotate_keep` | `5` | Positive bounded rotated-file count, excluding the live log; remove oldest when exceeded. |
| `logrotate_interval` | Unset in size mode | Optional daily/weekly/monthly time policy; cannot increase Scribe's actual run frequency. |
| `logrotate_compress` | On | Compression on/off using available gzip support. |
| `logrotate_delay_compress` | On | With compression, optionally leave newest rotation uncompressed until the next cycle. |
| `logrotate_max_age_days` | Off | Optional positive age limit; count retention works independently. |

### Fixed DDNS and IP-Sync policies

The fixed policy is shared in shape but generated separately for `ddns.log` and `ip-sync.log`. No rotation tuning prompts/menu/config overrides. Numeric defaults below are proposals to settle in P1/P6, not user-selected values or implemented guarantees.

| Fixed setting | Proposed value / behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Size trigger | `1M` for each file; positive fixed threshold, evaluated at Scribe's cadence. |
| History | Keep one numbered rotated file plus the live file; purge the oldest owned rotation. |
| Compression/time/age | Uncompressed, size-based only, no separate time/age policy or schedule. |
| File handling | Skip absent/empty files; preserve private permissions. Verify append/rotation concurrency before implementation. |
| Preferences | Separate proposed `ddns_logging_enabled` / `ip_sync_logging_enabled` and fixed-policy version/readiness. Auto-initialize on first fresh setup with ready Scribe; re-detect/enable validates the same defaults. No Scribe means no custom event file, while native messages continue. |

All three use numbered history: `cloudflared.log.1` through `.N` (and `.gz` when compressed), or the fixed `ddns.log.1` / `ip-sync.log.1`. Generate `nodateext`/`start 1` rather than inheriting date names. Size-only maps to `size`; cloudflared time-plus-size maps to the interval with `maxsize`. Use `missingok`/`notifempty`; verify directive support against installed logrotate, following the [upstream manual](https://github.com/logrotate/logrotate/blob/main/logrotate.8.in).

A size threshold is checked when Scribe runs; it is not an immediate hard cap. Show actual check cadence beside the policy. No CFMgr polling/rotation daemon, second cron, or manager-launch truncation. Altering Scribe's global schedule/system-message retention is outside initial scope.

### Fast check, repair, and explicit logging installation/removal

| Trigger/state | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Healthy manager launch | Check small saved state and owned files/fingerprints/include/prerequisite signals only for configured features with custom logging requested. Share base probes but retain per-feature results. No full dry-run, rotation, network check, dependency install, retry sleep, or restart. |
| No configured feature requesting custom logs | Skip automatic Scribe/rule checks entirely. Status/native messages still work as available. |
| Missing/stale owned fragment/include | Only for requested logging with usable Scribe and no cleanup guard, regenerate affected owned integration from saved/versioned policy; validate/commit atomically. Never recreate missing Scribe base or override opt-out; bounded work defers with notice. |
| Successful self-repair | Name the affected feature/rule, for example `Restored CFMgr DDNS logrotate rule.` Claim success only after validation/commit; deferred, unavailable, and failed are distinct. |
| Custom logging off | Skip automatic checks/repair for that feature. Its explicit re-detect is read-only: report availability and offer explicit enable without silently switching it on. Native messages stay active. |
| Explicit Install/enable logging | Configured feature only; review enable, validate policy/Scribe/storage/owned integration and resolve pending cleanup before activation. Re-detect may offer this action but cannot silently perform it. Never enable the service/other feature's logs or change Scribe runtime/schedule. |
| Explicit Uninstall logging | Available in selected configured feature submenu even when Scribe absent or unusable; save preference inactive and suppress writer/self-heal under shared guard before cleanup below. No prerequisite re-detect/heal or separate runtime logging-stop UI. |
| Explicit re-detect with logging on | Inside configured submenu run bounded complete validation/repair even if cache ready; preserve preference and report outcome. Never turn Off feature On. |
| Cloudflared policy change | Validate/stage/dry-run/commit config and owned rule together with recovery copies. Invalid changes preserve working policy. DDNS/IP-Sync expose no equivalent custom tuning. |
| `0) Check status` | Read and report enabled-feature observations/policy state only; do not run repair or full validation. Disabled features receive no runtime/logging details or probes. |

Proposed launch budget remains under one second when healthy, with at most two seconds of foreground check/repair across all three integrations; measure in P8. Use nonblocking locks and bounded validation; contention defers instead of waiting. Background hooks never prompt. Cache only results tied to current settings/files; missing/changed files invalidate the cache. No additional periodic repair job.

**Repair boundary:** only owned feature fragments, permissions, and include registration. For a valid top-level config missing coverage, add marked includes for the exact owned fragments after duplicate/directory/nested include checks and merged validation. Preserve other text/rules; do not edit `A01global` or native routing/retention. Missing/invalid Scribe base configuration/executables is unavailable, not something to reconstruct from guesses.

**Install/repair transaction:** inspect prerequisites/ownership, take documented nonblocking locks (including Scribe rotation lock only where relevant), retain recovery copies, generate/validate/commit. Only eligible current On/running Cloudflared may need controller restart when logfile arguments change; rule-only/native writer-policy changes need no tunnel restart or forced provider sync. Preserve service intent, other rules/native messages; failed install/repair rolls back safely. No automatic user archive or Scribe daemon/job/global-policy change.

### Per-feature Uninstall logging transaction

| Step / edge condition | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Reviewed intent first | After explicit review/Save, write scoped durable logging-cleanup transaction/generation guard and inactive logging preference BEFORE removing files/rules. Guard prevents watchdog/updater/start handlers respawning the writer during cleanup; suppress self-heal/new writes and quiesce old/inflight writers. Preserve configured/enabled/maintenance flags, policy values and operational hooks/jobs. Temporary stop is INTERNAL controller quiescence, never public cfmgr stop/Off handler; only owned LOG files/rules are removed. Failed/interrupted cleanup never rolls preference active or silently recreates logs. |
| Quiesce selected writer before deletion | A current verified owned Cloudflared process may still hold its open logfile despite saved opt-out/new launch arguments. Explicit cleanup authorizes bounded owned stop BEFORE deletion even if readiness fails or Scribe absent; this is an exception to readiness-before-ordinary-restart. Off is never enabled/started. Native jobs quiesce only owned append worker/inflight writer around deletion, no invented daemon/forced provider sync. Failed stop means no open-log deletion or false stop-success claim. |
| Found owned logs / rotations | Verify actual mounted writable Entware/resolved owned paths, then delete only found owned feature logfile/rotations with bounded inventory and symlink/foreign-root protections. This cleanup does not require Scribe readiness or presence. Preserve other logs/shared directory/native messages/manual archives. |
| Scribe detected, including failed readiness | On mounted writable storage remove independently verified owned rotation fragments that still exist. Edit surviving global include only when exact owned block is identifiable and whole-file merge/syntax/concurrency checks pass; otherwise report residual/deferred cleanup and preserve foreign text. Never create/repair missing Scribe base/includes, edit A01global, or change Scribe daemon/job/schedule/global policy. Its own uninstall may already have removed rules; report found/absent accurately. |
| Scribe fully absent | Skip Scribe config edits/repair/re-detect/heal; inactive preference/writer quiescence and found owned log deletion still proceed. A vanished rule is already absent, never recreated for removal. |
| Resume after safe writer quiescence | Once the old writer is safely quiesced and inactive logging preference durably committed, only still-enabled valid current-generation Cloudflared restarts through existing readiness controller WITHOUT logfile arguments. Readiness/start failure is reported waiting/deferred/failed, not success; saved On remains eligible for gated watchdog recovery without file logging. Native jobs resume eligible existing work without file output, no forced sync. Off remains Off. |
| Missing/read-only/lost Entware or stop failure | Keep saved logging preference/self-heal inactive and durable cleanup metadata; no stale /opt writes or deletion-success claim. Defer unsafe file/rule deletion. Failure to quiesce an existing open writer keeps the temporary execution guard and reports runtime cleanup incomplete, never claimed stopped because preference changed. Attempt verified owned bounded temporary stop independently of Scribe presence; no open-log deletion until safe. Native writers honor inactive preference independent of mount. |
| Temporary execution guard versus pending log cleanup | Hold the scoped execution guard until owned writer quiescence and durable opt-out are verified. Then release that temporary runtime gate while retaining pending file/rule cleanup metadata and inactive logging preference. Residual log cleanup alone cannot block eligible native DDNS/IP-Sync API work or system messages, or ready Cloudflared running without file logging. Report removal incomplete until pending deletion resolves; a pending cleanup record never authorizes file output, self-heal or logging re-enable. |
| Completion / later enable | Report actual writer/rule/file outcomes; release temporary execution gate as above and clear pending cleanup metadata only after required file/rule cleanup safely resolves. Retain opt-out and original operational hooks/jobs/activation intent; ready same intended enabled service resumes without file flags even if safe residual cleanup is pending.
